Maybe use " Leite" iterative algorithm: Start flat q target, use inverse square root spectral factor with positivity projection. Let's explore this more; could give very flat sequence.

Given desired q (convolution) flat-ish, take FFT, compute a = inverse FFT of 0.5 log FFT(q)? If q is nonnegative and nonnegative time, its DFT may have zeros/phase. But we can set a_k = exp(0.5 log Q_k) with phase, then inverse, project positive. This is cepstrum smoothing. Or more directly: choose A(ω) to have magnitude sqrt(|Q|) and phase adjusted to produce nonnegative a. We can optimize phase via iterative: alternate between time-domain nonnegative projection and frequency-domain magnitude constraint A^2=Q. This is like phase retrieval. Let's design local search in time domain more advanced:
- Use smooth max log-sum-exp objective with adaptive temperature. Instead of just gradient topK, use multiplicative projected gradient with backtracking and restarts.

Coordinate descent for nonconvex objective: For one coordinate a_i, c_k changes linearly/quadratically? c_k includes a_i terms: for each k, if a_j with i+j=k, c_k = old + a_i*(a_j) + maybe a_i^2 for i=i? Actually derivative linear in a_i. Objective as function of a_i (one variable, others fixed) is max of linear+a_i^2 if self pair? c_k = c_k0 + a_i*(a_{k-i}) + (if i+j=2i? one term? derivative includes possibly). The maximum of several quadratics? Since a_i appears in each c_k linearly times partner plus for k=2i a_i^2. For k where both indices involve i, term is a_i * a_j; if i=j, a_i^2. So c_k is quadratic in a_i (convex). Max of convex is convex; can minimize 1D convex by ternary/golden or derivative. This can refine exactly! For a set of coordinates, small QP. What about using `cvxpy` for a convex surrogate? We can formulate minimize logsumexp of linearized c? If we approximate c_k by current linearization: c_k^lin = c_k + ∑(2a_{k-i})Δa. Then constraints c_k^lin ≤ M linear. This is cutting-plane convex. We can use linear programming/second-order? At each iteration, solve convex problem min M s.t. linearized c≤M, sum=1, a≥0. This is a linear program! Because linearized convolution is linear in a, objective max linear functions -> LP. This is like an outer approximation of the nonconvex max. It may converge to a local optimum. We can solve with scipy linprog or highs! Variables n, constraints 2n-1 for each lag: c_k + ∑ g_{k,i} (a_i - a_i0) ≤ M? Introduce M variable. LP:
minimize M
subject to ∑_{i} g_{k,i} a_i + (c_k - ∑ g_{k,i} a_i0) ≤ M for all k
∑a_i=1, a_i≥0.
Where g_{k,i}=2 a_current_{k-i} (valid). This is a linearization around current a. Since c is bilinear not convex, linearization underestimates/ over? For convex? If we solve LP, we get new a with lower linearized max. We can update a (convex combination) and re-linearize, like linear outer approximation for nonconvex? Not guaranteed but could improve. This resembles previous state of art LP but dual? Previous LP had constraints f*g≤M (linear in g with fixed f), similar but different: It used cross product of fixed f and new g, not self product. The linearization above uses 2 a_current in gradient, i.e., tangent plane. For bilinear squares, tangent plane may over/under. But LP can be solved with HiGHS sparse for n~512. This might be strong: It minimizes a linear surrogate of max conv, with nonneg and sum. It will naturally choose a to reduce predicted peaks. We can mix to ensure descent. Let's refine SLP implementation and performance.
`slp_solve_lp(a)`:
- Input a normalized sum=1 (nonneg).
- c = conv_fft(a); M = max c (current).
- L=2n-1.
- Allocate A = np.zeros((L, n+1), dtype=float64). For memory, if n>1536 maybe use sparse? We'll restrict SLP n<=1200 dense. For n=1024 okay. For n=512 fine.

`fast_convolution(a)` details:
```
def fast_convolution(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.empty(0)
    L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (L-1).bit_length()
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:L]
    c=np.ascontiguousarray(c)
    np.maximum(c,0.0,out=c)
    return c
```
For n large >1<<? N maybe for n=4096 L=8191 N=8192. For n=8192 N=16384. OK.

def conv_segment(a, w):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return empty
    L=len(w) # should 2n-1
    N=1 << (n + L -2).bit_length()? conv length n+L-1=3n-2; need N>=3n-1. For w length 2n-1, conv length 3n-2. But we only need middle n coefficients. Can use zero padding N >= 3n-2. fa=rfft(a_rev,N); fw=rfft(w,N); c=irfft[:3n-2]; return c[n-1:2n-1]? Wait earlier index n-1..2n-2 inclusive length n. Conv length = 3n-2, last index 3n-3. We need k = n-1 + i for i=0..n-1 => indices n-1..2n-2. Good.
```

```
def weighted_window(a, w):
    n=a.size; L=2*n-1
    N=1 << (3*n-2 -1).bit_length()? If len=3n-2, need N>=len. `need=n + len(w) -1`. N=1 << (need-1).bit_length()
    ar=a[::-1]
    # ensure ar contiguous
    ar=np.ascontiguousarray(ar)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(ar,N)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,N)[:need]
    return c[n-1:2*n-1]
```
Check with n=2, a=[a0,a1], w0,w1,w2. ar=[a1,a0]. conv: index1: a1*w0 + a0*w1? Our target h_0 = ∑_j a_j w_j? For i=0, h_0 = w0 a0 + w1 a1? Wait h_i =∑_{j=0}^{n-1} a_j w_{i+j}. For i=0: a0 w0 + a1 w1. conv(ar,w) index n-1=1: ar0*w1 + ar1*w0 = a1*w1 + a0*w0. same. Good. h_1 index2: ar0*w2 + ar1*w1 = a1*w2 + a0*w1. Target i=1: a0 w1 + a1 w2. good.

`slp_local_search` complete:
```
def slp_local_search(a, budget_s, seed=0, max_slp_iters=200):
    a=normalize(a,1)
    c=fast_conv; score=...
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    t0=time; stagnant=0; iter=0
    while time < deadline and stagnant<10:
       iter++
       a_lp = slp_solve_lp(a)
       if a_lp is None: break
       eval candidates: t_values=[1.0,0.7,0.5,0.3,0.2,0.1,0.05]
       best_cand=a; best_cand_score=score
       for t: if t<=0 break; cand=(1-t)*a + t*a_lp; normalize; sc=fast_score; if sc<best_cand_score: ...
       if best_cand_score < best_score - 1e-12: accept current=best_cand; best=... stagnant=0
       else:
           # if current a is in candidates maybe score same; try flat_log
           cand=flat_log_step(a, 0.3); sc=...
           if sc<best_score*(1-1e-12): accept ...
           else stagnant++
       # occasionally perturb if stagnant 5
       if stagnant>8: a=smooth_perturb(best, rng,0.2); ...
    return best,best_score
```
Need if LP solution equals a (score same), flat may help. If no improvement many, break.

Now, CLIP: The evaluator clips values at 1000. Our normalized sum=1 sequences max maybe <1; fine. For sanitize return scale to target sqrt(2n). If max*target >1000, choose target =1000/max. ```
seq=...; s=sum; if s<0.01 -> fallback
target=math.sqrt(2*n)
scale=target/s
if seq.max()*scale >1000: scale=1000/seq.max()
seq=seq*scale
```
This ensures valid.

- Aeq=np.ones(n+1); Aeq[n]=0
- bounds=[(0.0, None)]*n + [(0.0, None)]

- if res.success: x=res.x; x=np.asarray(x,float); x=np.where(np.isfinite(x),x,0); x=np.clip(x,0,1000); s=sum; if s>0 x/=s; else None
- else None

Let's implement `smooth_perturb(a, rng, strength)`:
```
for _ in range(max(1, min(3, n//1000))):
   t=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   z=np.zeros(n)
   for m in range(1,4): amp=strength/(m); ph=rng.uniform(0,2π); z += amp*np.cos(2π m t + ph)
   a *= np.exp(z)
   if rng.random()<0.3: width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//100), max(2,n//5+1))); start=...; a[start:stop]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength,stop-start))
return normalize(a,1)
```

`run_level` code:
```
def run_level(n, budget_s, pool, seed=0, use_slp=True, random_count=20):
    if budget_s < 10: return returns top pool upsampled
    rng=default_rng(seed)
    starts=[]
    for sc,seq in pool[:8]: add upsample
    for _ in range(random_count): ...
    sort
    best_seq=None; best_score=inf
    k = min(3, len(starts), max(1, int(budget_s/70)+1))
    budget_each=budget_s/k
    for idx in range(k):
       time_this = min(budget_each, deadline-now-2)
       if time_this <=2: break
       sc,sq = starts[idx]
       if sc < best_score: ...
       if use_slp and n <= MAX_SLP_N and time_this > 40:
          max_iter = 800 if n<=300 else 300 if n<=512 else 100
          slp_budget = min(time_this*0.75, 600)
          seq,score = slp_local_search(sq, slp_budget, seed=seed+idx, max_iters=max_iter)
       else:
          seq,score = gradient_polish(sq,time_this,seed=seed+idx)
       # If SLP ran, small gradient polish remaining if >10
       if use_slp and n<=MAX_SLP_N and time_this - slp_budget > 15:
          seq,score2=gradient_polish(seq, time_this - slp_budget, seed=...)
          if score2 < score: ...
       update best/pool
    if best_seq None: use best start
    return best_seq,best_score
```
Need `deadline = time.monotonic()+budget_s` inside? Since caller has global. Use local deadline.
If SLP budget `time_this*0.75` but if time_this small, skip.
`slp_budget` may exceed time_this? no.

For final gradient levels, use `use_slp=False` to avoid SLP.

Global pool update in propose: after each run_level, `add_pool(pool, seq, score)`. Also update `global_best`.

At the very start, add `height_sequence_1` maybe length 512. It might not be normalized sum; fast_score handles. If length not exact? ok.

Now, if previous best 1.523 and our SLP fails, final returns maybe previous. Need meaningful improvement; hope.

Let's think if there is any risk LP finds a direction that lowers predicted max but increases actual due second order and line search chooses t small, leading to very slow convergence. We can also use a "quadratic correction": after LP solve, update `a_new=(1-t)*a+t*a_lp` and then project. For small t, second-order ~t^2, so many iters. But 300 iters at 5% each could move enough. If LP direction remains same, after 100 iters 0.6 mix. Good.

Could we instead solve a trust-region quadratic subproblem? For each k, c_k ≈ linear + 1/2 δ^T H_k δ. Max of quadratics nonconvex. Not.

Alternative: Use the LP solution as a target and run a line search with larger t using exact score; if t=1 bad but t=0.5 good, accepted. If only t=0.02 good, slow. Maybe add "projection" direction: after LP, compute `d = a_lp/(sum a_lp) - a`, then do regularized gradient descent with relaxation? SLP mix already.

Maybe use `a_new = a + t*(a_lp-a)` with t backtracking; if t=1 nonfinite or score worse. We already try 1,0.7,0.5. Good.

Now, if SLP starts from poor uniform, first LP may zero endpoints and produce sequence with sum1 but maybe actual score significantly worse at t=1, t=0.1 improves? It should. If no improvement, flat update maybe. Uniform uniform SLP should move.

Let's add a cheap local coordinate descent? Maybe not.

Another thought: For nonnegative a, the matrix B in LP has rows with zeros at endpoints. This allows endpoints zero. But true optimum may have small but positive endpoints. Multiplicative gradient from SLP with floor maintains. Good.

Now, final answer code. We'll include comments but not excessive. Ensure no `lambda`. Use `math` for sqrt.

Let's write final code now.
</think>

```python
"""
Multi-stage search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a))**2.

The algorithm uses:
- FFT-based exact-enough convolution for objective evaluation.
- Sequential linear programming (SLP): linearize each convolution
  coefficient around the current point and solve an LP to minimize the
  linearized maximum. Line-search mixing ensures descent.
- Multiplicative smooth-max gradient steps and a fast log-flattening update.
- A coarse-to-fine plan: parameterized starts at small lengths, SLP at
  medium lengths, high-resolution polishing, and final exact validation.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None

MAX_SLP_N = 600
POOL_CAP = 24


def _score_key(pair):
    return pair[0]


def normalize_sequence(a, target=1.0, max_value=1000.0):
    """Clip to [0, max_value] and scale to target sum (if possible)."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([float(max(target, 1.0) / 1000.0)], dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)

    for _ in range(4):
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15:
            a.fill(float(target) / float(a.size))
            a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)
            return a
        a *= float(target) / s
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)

    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a.fill(1.0)
    else:
        a *= target / s

    return np.clip(a, 0.0, max_value)


def fast_convolution(a):
    """Linear convolution a * a, truncated to length 2n-1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if n <= 96:
        c = np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
        c = np.ascontiguousarray(c)
        np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
        return c

    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = 1 << (L - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    c = np.ascontiguousarray(c)
    np.maximum(c, 0.0, out=c)
    return c


def fast_score_array(a):
    """Fast scale-invariant analogue of evaluate_sequence()."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")

    c = fast_convolution(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")

    m = float(c.max())
    if not math.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def weighted_window(a, w):
    """
    Compute h_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j} for i=0..n-1, where w has length 2n-1.
    Used for smooth-max gradient / KKT-like updates.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0 or w.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w), w, 0.0)

    need = n + w.size - 1
    N = 1 << max(0, (need - 1).bit_length())
    ar = np.ascontiguousarray(a[::-1])
    fa = np.fft.rfft(ar, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, N)[:need]
    out = c[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    return np.ascontiguousarray(np.where(np.isfinite(out), out, 0.0))


def smooth_weights(c, beta):
    """
    Softmax subgradient weights for a smoothed maximum of c.
    beta small = global smoothing, beta large = near max-active set.
    """
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    L = c.size
    if L == 0:
        return None
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if cmax <= 1e-300 or beta <= 1e-8:
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)

    z = beta * (c - cmax) / cmax
    np.clip(z, -700.0, 0.0, out=z)
    w = np.exp(z)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(L, 1.0 / L, dtype=np.float64)
    w /= s
    return w


def flat_log_step(a, alpha=0.25, log_clip=4.0):
    """
    Multiplicative update trying to flatten convolution coefficients toward
    their average. Very cheap after convolution.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a

    a = normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)
    c = fast_convolution(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1

    s = float(a.sum())
    target = (s * s) / float(max(1, L))
    if target <= 1e-300:
        return a

    d = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    np.divide(target, c + 1e-300, out=d)
    np.log(d, out=d)
    np.clip(d, -log_clip, log_clip, out=d)

    cs = np.empty(L + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cs[0] = 0.0
    cs[1:] = np.cumsum(d)
    b = cs[n:] - cs[:-n]

    u = b - float(b.mean())
    denom = max(1, n - 1)
    u /= float(denom)

    # Keep multiplicative step tame.
    np.clip(u, -3.0, 3.0, out=u)
    cand = a * np.exp(alpha * u)
    cand = np.maximum(cand, 0.0)
    return normalize_sequence(cand, 1.0)


def smooth_perturbation(a, rng, strength=0.25):
    """Smooth/random multiplicative perturbation used for restarts."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a

    a = normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, 4):
        amp = strength / (0.7 + 0.5 * m)
        ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)

    a *= np.exp(z)

    if n > 8 and rng.random() < 0.4:
        width = int(rng.integers(max(1, n // 200), max(2, n // 8 + 1)))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)
        if stop > start:
            a[start:stop] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, strength, size=stop - start))

    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    return normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)


def make_beta_sequence(n, alpha, beta, eps=1.0):
    """a_i proportional to (i+eps)^alpha * (n-i+eps)^beta."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64), 1.0)

    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + float(eps)
    y = (n - 1 - np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)) + float(eps)

    with np.errstate(over="ignore", under="ignore", invalid="ignore", divide="ignore"):
        a = np.power(x, float(alpha)) * np.power(y, float(beta))

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    return normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)


def make_fourier_sequence(n, rng, max_freq=8, max_amp=1.8):
    """Low-frequency log-density positive sequence."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64), 1.0)

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for m in range(1, max_freq + 1):
        amp = rng.normal(0.0, max_amp / (0.6 + 0.55 * m))
        ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi)
        z += amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * m * t + ph)

    z -= float(z.max())
    a = np.exp(z)
    return normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)


def make_simple_shape(n, kind, rng=None):
    """A handful of deterministic shapes."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(max(1, n)), 1.0)

    t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    if kind == "flat":
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    elif kind == "linear":
        a = np.linspace(1e-3, 1.0, n)
    elif kind == "linear_neg":
        a = np.linspace(1.0, 1e-3, n)
    elif kind == "quad":
        a = t * t + 1e-4
    elif kind == "quad_neg":
        a = (1.0 - t) ** 2 + 1e-4
    elif kind == "cubic":
        a = t * t * (1.0 + 0.5 * t) + 1e-4
    elif kind == "cubic_neg":
        a = (1.0 - t) ** 2 * (1.0 + 0.5 * (1.0 - t)) + 1e-4
    elif kind == "beta1":
        a = make_beta_sequence(n, 1.0, 0.0)
    elif kind == "beta2":
        a = make_beta_sequence(n, 2.0, 0.3)
    elif kind == "beta3":
        a = make_beta_sequence(n, 0.5, 1.2)
    elif kind == "beta4":
        a = make_beta_sequence(n, 1.5, 1.5)
    elif kind == "sin":
        a = np.sin(np.pi * t) ** 2 + 1e-4
    elif kind == "cos_half":
        a = np.cos(0.5 * np.pi * t) ** 2 + 1e-4
    else:
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

    return normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)


def upsample_sequence(seq, n):
    """Linearly interpolate to length n and renormalize."""
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    m = int(seq.size)
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n) * float(seq.mean() if m else 1.0), 1.0)

    if m == 0:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), 1.0)
    if m == n:
        return normalize_sequence(seq, 1.0)
    if m == 1:
        return normalize_sequence(np.ones(n) * float(seq[0]), 1.0)

    xold = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    xnew = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    out = np.interp(xnew, xold, seq)
    return normalize_sequence(out, 1.0)


def add_candidate_pool(pool, seq, score):
    """Maintain a small sorted pool of (score, sequence)."""
    try:
        sc = float(score)
    except Exception:
        return
    if seq is None or not math.isfinite(sc):
        return

    arr = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.size == 0:
        arr = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
    arr = normalize_sequence(arr, 1.0)
    pool.append((sc, arr))
    pool.sort(key=_score_key)
    while len(pool) > POOL_CAP:
        pool.pop()


def param_seed_search(n, budget_s, rng):
    """
    Generate many low-dimensional starts. This is cheap at small n and helps
    escape the local basin found by previous approaches.
    """
    n = int(n)
    budget_s = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + budget_s

    local = []

    simple_kinds = [
        "flat", "linear", "linear_neg", "quad", "quad_neg",
        "cubic", "cubic_neg", "beta1", "beta2", "beta3", "beta4",
        "sin", "cos_half"
    ]
    for k in simple_kinds:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 0.1:
            break
        sq = make_simple_shape(n, k, rng)
        add_candidate_pool(local, sq, fast_score_array(sq))

    alphas = [0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, 3.0, 3.5]
    for aa in alphas:
        for bb in alphas[:8]:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 0.2:
                break
            sq = make_beta_sequence(n, aa, bb, eps=0.75)
            add_candidate_pool(local, sq, fast_score_array(sq))

    count = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.5:
        count += 1
        if count % 500 == 0:
            best = local[0][0] if local else float("inf")
            print(f"  param search n={n} iter={count} best={best:.6f}")

        if rng.random() < 0.55:
            sq = make_beta_sequence(
                n,
                rng.uniform(-0.4, 3.5),
                rng.uniform(-0.4, 3.5),
                eps=float(rng.choice([0.5, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5]))
            )
        else:
            sq = make_fourier_sequence(
                n,
                rng,
                max_freq=int(rng.integers(4, 10)),
                max_amp=float(rng.uniform(0.8, 2.8))
            )
        add_candidate_pool(local, sq, fast_score_array(sq))

    if not local:
        local.append((2.0, normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), 1.0)))
    return local[:12]


def slp_solve_lp(a):
    """
    Solve one sequential linear programming step.

    For current a0, minimize M subject to:
        sum_i 2*a0_{k-i} a_i - M <= c_k(a0), for all k
        sum_i a_i = 1, a_i >= 0, M >= 0.

    This is the first-order tangent to the max-convolution objective.
    """
    if linprog is None:
        return None

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0 or n > MAX_SLP_N:
        return None

    a = normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)
    c = fast_convolution(a)
    L = 2 * n - 1

    # Dense matrix is okay for n <= 600.
    A = np.zeros((L, n + 1), dtype=np.float64)
    A[:, n] = -1.0

    v = 2.0 * a
    # Column i has a_i's coefficient 2*a0_{k-i} for rows k=i..i+n-1.
    for i in range(n):
        A[i:i + n, i] = v

    b = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    np.maximum(b, 0.0, out=b)

    obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    obj[n] = 1.0

    Aeq = np.ones(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    Aeq[n] = 0.0
    beq = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n + [(0.0, None)]

    try:
        res = linprog(
            obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b,
            A_eq=Aeq,
            b_eq=beq,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True}
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if getattr(res, "success", False):
        x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        x = x[:n]
        x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
        x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(x.sum())
        if s > 1e-12:
            x /= s
            return normalize_sequence(x, 1.0)
    return None


def slp_local_search(a, budget_s, seed=0, max_iters=500):
    """
    Run SLP with line-search mixing. If SLP stalls, use a log-flattening
    move. This is the main global local-refinement engine at medium n.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    a = normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)
    score = fast_score_array(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    if not math.isfinite(score):
        return best, best_score

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    stagnant = 0
    fails = 0

    mix_values = (1.0, 0.75, 0.5, 0.35, 0.22, 0.12, 0.06, 0.025)
    iter_no = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2 and stagnant < 18 and fails < 8:
        iter_no += 1
        a_work = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
        a_work = normalize_sequence(a_work, 1.0)

        a_lp = slp_solve_lp(a_work)
        if a_lp is None:
            fails += 1
            # Fall back to flattening temporarily.
            cand = flat_log_step(a, 0.25)
            sc = fast_score_array(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = cand.copy()
                best_score = sc
            elif math.isfinite(sc) and sc < fast_score_array(a) * 1.01:
                a = cand.copy()
            stagnant += 1
            continue

        current_score = fast_score_array(a)
        cand_best = a.copy()
        cand_best_score = current_score

        for t in mix_values:
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 0.2:
                break
            cand = (1.0 - t) * a + t * a_lp
            cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-12)
            cand = normalize_sequence(cand, 1.0)
            sc = fast_score_array(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < cand_best_score:
                cand_best = cand
                cand_best_score = sc

        if math.isfinite(cand_best_score) and cand_best_score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best = cand_best.copy()
            best_score = cand_best_score
            a = cand_best
            stagnant = 0
            if iter_no % 25 == 0:
                print(f"    SLP n={n} iter={iter_no} best={best_score:.6f}")
        else:
            # Try a flattening escape move.
            esc = flat_log_step(a, 0.25)
            esc_score = fast_score_array(esc)
            if math.isfinite(esc_score) and esc_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = esc.copy()
                best_score = esc_score
                a = esc
                stagnant = 0
            elif math.isfinite(esc_score) and esc_score < current_score * 1.01:
                a = esc
                stagnant += 1
            else:
                stagnant += 1
                if stagnant >= 9:
                    a = smooth_perturbation(best, rng, 0.22)
                    score = fast_score_array(a)
                    if score < best_score:
                        best = a.copy()
                        best_score = score
                    stagnant = 0
        if iter_no >= int(max_iters):
            break

    return best, best_score


def gradient_polish(a, budget_s, seed=0):
    """
    High-resolution polishing using smooth-max multiplicative updates and
    occasional log-flattening moves.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    a = normalize_sequence(a, 1.0)
    score = fast_score_array(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    if not math.isfinite(score):
        return best, best_score

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    eta = 0.12
    beta = 2.0
    accept_rel = 0.025
    stagnant = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2 and stagnant < 220:
        it += 1
        if not math.isfinite(score):
            a = best.copy()
            score = best_score

        c = fast_convolution(a)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-15:
            a = best.copy()
            score = best_score
            continue

        score = float(2.0 * n * float(c.max()) / (s * s))
        if math.isfinite(score) and score < best_score - 1e-12 + 1e-15:
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = score
            stagnant = 0

        # Cool / anneal temperature a little.
        beta = 2.0 + 7.0 * min(1.0, it / 600.0)
        if it % 160 == 0:
            beta = 1.5 + rng.uniform(0.0, 4.0)

        w = smooth_weights(c, beta)
        if w is not None and w.size == c.size:
            h = weighted_window(a, w)
            if h.size == n and math.isfinite(float(h.sum())):
                h = h - float(h.mean())
                scale = float(np.max(np.abs(h))) if h.size else 0.0
                if scale > 1e-15:
                    logf = -eta * (h / scale)
                    if it % 12 == 0:
                        logf += rng.normal(0.0, 0.01, n)
                    np.clip(logf, -2.5, 2.5, out=logf)
                    cand = a * np.exp(logf)
                    cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-12)
                    cand = normalize_sequence(cand, 1.0)
                    cand_score = fast_score_array(cand)

                    if math.isfinite(cand_score) and cand_score < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best = cand.copy()
                        best_score = cand_score
                    if math.isfinite(cand_score) and cand_score <= score * (1.0 + accept_rel) + 1e-14:
                        a = cand
                        score = cand_score
                        eta = min(0.65, eta * 1.035)
                        accept_rel = max(0.0015, accept_rel * 0.999)
                        stagnant = 0
                        if it % 75 == 0:
                            print(f"    grad n={n} iter={it} best={best_score:.6f}")
                    else:
                        eta = max(0.02, eta * 0.82)
                        stagnant += 1
                else:
                    stagnant += 1
            else:
                stagnant += 1
        else:
            stagnant += 1

        # Periodic flattening escape.
        if stagnant % 18 == 0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.5:
            cand = flat_log_step(a, 0.22)
            sc = fast_score_array(cand)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = cand.copy()
                best_score = sc
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc <= score * 1.02:
                a = cand
                score = sc
                eta = min(0.5, eta * 1.08)
                stagnant = 0
            else:
                stagnant += 1

        if stagnant > 80:
            a = smooth_perturbation(best, rng, 0.25)
            score = fast_score_array(a)
            eta = 0.12
            beta = 2.5
            accept_rel = 0.03
            stagnant = 0

    return best, best_score


def run_level(n, budget_s, pool, seed=0, use_slp=True, random_starts=20):
    """
    For a fixed length n: build starts from the pool and fresh random starts,
    then refine several promising ones.
    """
    n = int(n)
    budget_s = max(5.0, float(budget_s))
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + budget_s

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    starts = []

    # Carry forward the best known sequences.
    for sc, seq in pool[:8]:
        starts.append((fast_score_array(upsample_sequence(seq, n)), upsample_sequence(seq, n)))

    # Fresh starts, somewhat less for large n because local search is enough.
    if n <= 300:
        rs = max(8, int(random_starts))
    elif n <= 1000:
        rs = max(5, int(random_starts * 0.6))
    else:
        rs = max(3, int(random_starts * 0.35))

    for _ in range(rs):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 2.0:
            break
        if rng.random() < 0.55:
            sq = make_beta_sequence(
                n,
                rng.uniform(-0.3, 3.0),
                rng.uniform(-0.3, 3.0),
                eps=float(rng.choice([0.5, 0.75, 1.0]))
            )
        else:
            sq = make_fourier_sequence(
                n,
                rng,
                max_freq=int(rng.integers(5, 11)),
                max_amp=float(rng.uniform(0.7, 2.4))
            )
        starts.append((fast_score_array(sq), normalize_sequence(sq, 1.0)))

    # Deterministic anchors.
    for k in ("flat", "linear", "quad", "beta2", "sin"):
        sq = make_simple_shape(n, k, rng)
        starts.append((fast_score_array(sq), normalize_sequence(sq, 1.0)))

    starts = [x for x in starts if x is not None and math.isfinite(x[0])]
    starts.sort(key=_score_key)

    if not starts:
        starts.append((2.0, normalize_sequence(np.ones(n), 1.0)))

    best_seq = starts[0][1].copy()
    best_score = starts[0][0]
    add_candidate_pool(pool, best_seq, best_score)

    remain = max(0.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    k = min(3, len(starts), max(1, int(remain / 90.0) + 1))
    time_each = max(5.0, remain / float(k))

    for i in range(k):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 3.0:
            break

        time_this = max(5.0, min(time_each, deadline - time.monotonic() - 2.0))
        if time_this <= 0:
            break

        sc, sq = starts[i]
        sq = normalize_sequence(sq, 1.0)

        if sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = sq.copy()
            add_candidate_pool(pool, best_seq, best_score)

        slp_used = False
        if use_slp and n <= MAX_SLP_N and time_this > 35.0:
            slp_time = min(time_this * 0.78, 700.0)
            max_iter = 900 if n <= 320 else 420
            seq, score = slp_local_search(
                sq,
                slp_time,
                seed=int(seed * 1000 + i * 17 + 3),
                max_iters=max_iter
            )
            slp_used = True
        else:
            seq, score = gradient_polish(sq, time_this, seed=int(seed * 1000 + i * 23 + 7))

        if slp_used and time_this > slp_time + 12.0:
            rem = max(8.0, time_this - slp_time)
            seq2, score2 = gradient_polish(seq, rem, seed=int(seed * 1000 + i * 31 + 11))
            if score2 < score:
                seq, score = seq2, score2

        if math.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            print(f"  level n={n} candidate improved: {best_score:.6f}")
            add_candidate_pool(pool, best_seq, best_score)

    return best_seq, best_score


def sanitize_for_return(seq):
    """Return a valid plain Python list of nonnegative finite floats."""
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size == 0:
        return [1.0]

    seq = np.where(np.isfinite(seq), seq, 0.0)
    seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(seq.sum())

    if s < 0.01:
        out = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64)
        return [float(x) for x in out]

    n = int(seq.size)
    target = math.sqrt(2.0 * float(n))
    scale = target / s

    mx = float(seq.max())
    if mx > 1e-300 and mx * scale > 1000.0:
        scale = 1000.0 / mx

    seq = seq * scale
    seq = np.clip(seq, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if float(seq.sum()) < 0.01:
        return [1.0] * 1024

    return [float(x) for x in seq]


def propose_candidate(budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point. Uses a coarse-to-fine plan:
      1. parameterized seed search at small n;
      2. SLP + polishing at 256/512;
      3. gradient/flat polishing at 1024;
      4. optional very long high-resolution polishing;
      5. exact final validation of top few candidates.
    """
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))
    seed = 1234567

    if budget < 20.0:
        return sanitize_for_return(np.ones(512))

    print(f"propose_candidate budget={budget:.1f}s")

    # Leave reserve for exact checking and cleanup.
    search_deadline = t0 + max(10.0, budget - 75.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pool = []

    # Use provided previous best if present.
    try:
        prev = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if prev is not None:
            arr = np.asarray(prev, dtype=np.float64)
            if arr.size > 0:
                sc = fast_score_array(arr)
                if math.isfinite(sc):
                    add_candidate_pool(pool, normalize_sequence(arr, 1.0), sc)
                    print(f"initial height_sequence_1 fast score={sc:.6f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Safe fallback candidate.
    fall = normalize_sequence(np.ones(512), 1.0)
    fall_score = fast_score_array(fall)
    add_candidate_pool(pool, fall, fall_score)
    global_best_seq = fall.copy()
    global_best_score = fall_score

    def maybe_update(seq, score):
        nonlocal global_best_seq, global_best_score
        if seq is not None and math.isfinite(score) and score < global_best_score:
            global_best_seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            global_best_score = float(score)
            print(f"global best updated: {global_best_score:.6f}")
            add_candidate_pool(pool, global_best_seq, global_best_score)

    # 1. Small-n seed search.
    if search_deadline - time.monotonic() > 90.0:
        n_small = 256
        b_small = min(240.0, 0.11 * budget)
        seeds = param_seed_search(n_small, b_small, rng)
        for sc, sq in seeds:
            maybe_update(sq, sc)
        print(f"after seed search n={n_small} best={global_best_score:.6f}")

        if search_deadline - time.monotonic() > 130.0:
            seq, sc = run_level(n_small, min(160.0, 0.07 * budget), pool, seed=seed + 11, use_slp=True, random_starts=10)
            maybe_update(seq, sc)

    # 2. Medium lengths: main SLP engine.
    if search_deadline - time.monotonic() > 220.0:
        n_mid = 512
        b_mid = min(1050.0, 0.42 * budget)
        seq, sc = run_level(n_mid, b_mid, pool, seed=seed + 101, use_slp=True, random_starts=18)
        maybe_update(seq, sc)
        print(f"after n=512 best={global_best_score:.6f}")

    # If a lot of time is left and n=512 already looks close, do another 512
    # pass from refreshed random starts (helps jump basins).
    if search_deadline - time.monotonic() > 300.0 and global_best_score > 1.495:
        n_mid2 = 512
        b_mid2 = min(350.0, 0.14 * budget)
        seq, sc = run_level(n_mid2, b_mid2, pool, seed=seed + 777, use_slp=True, random_starts=14)
        maybe_update(seq, sc)

    # 3. High-resolution gradient/flatten polishing.
    if search_deadline - time.monotonic() > 170.0:
        n_high = 1024
        b_high = min(800.0, 0.30 * budget)
        seq, sc = run_level(n_high, b_high, pool, seed=seed + 909, use_slp=False, random_starts=6)
        maybe_update(seq, sc)
        print(f"after n=1024 best={global_best_score:.6f}")

    # 4. Very high resolution if time remains.
    rem = search_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 120.0:
        final_n = 2048 if budget < 1700 else 4096
        seq, sc = run_level(final_n, rem, pool, seed=seed + 31415, use_slp=False, random_starts=3)
        maybe_update(seq, sc)
        print(f"after final n={final_n} best={global_best_score:.6f}")

    # 5. Exact validation of a few top candidates.
    # Use current time, not original deadline, to avoid overshoot.
    exact_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(10.0, min(60.0, budget - 25.0))
    exact_best_seq = global_best_seq
    exact_best_score = global_best_score

    if linprog is None:
        pass

    pool.sort(key=_score_key)
    checked = 0
    for sc, seq in pool[:5]:
        if time.monotonic() >= exact_deadline:
            break
        try:
            L = int(np.asarray(seq).size)
            if L > 6000:
                continue
            as_list = sanitize_for_return(seq)
            ex = float(evaluate_sequence(as_list))
        except Exception:
            ex = float("inf")
        if math.isfinite(ex) and ex < exact_best_score:
            exact_best_score = ex
            exact_best_seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
            checked += 1
        if checked >= 3:
            break

    # Final safeguard.
    if exact_best_seq is None or not math.isfinite(exact_best_score):
        exact_best_seq = global_best_seq if global_best_seq is not None else np.ones(512)
        exact_best_score = fast_score_array(exact_best_seq)

    out = sanitize_for_return(exact_best_seq)
    try:
        final_exact = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
    except Exception:
        final_exact = float("inf")

    if math.isfinite(final_exact) and final_exact < exact_best_score:
        exact_best_score = final_exact

    print(f"returned fast bound~{exact_best_score:.6f}, exact~{final_exact:.6f}, len={len(out)}")
    return out
```
